# ONECALL project execution control

> Controller: Phaseflow
>
> Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md`
>
> Branch: `codex/pa3-v003-subject-number-and-claim-foundation`
>
> Dispatch protocol: `sub-agents` via one-shot runner processes
>
> Dispatch providers: `mimo26flash` using `mimo-v2.6-flash` is the default implementation/fix author and a fresh `ds` task using API model ID `deepseek-flash` (DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash) is the default code reviewer/re-reviewer. DeepSeek becomes the code author only when the user explicitly requests it or MiMo fails under the bounded retry rule. Plan, investigation and document tasks remain gate-selected. `mimo26` uses `mimo-v2.6-pro`. All use `claude-agent-run` while the Codex Responses adapter cannot complete write-tool result correlation.

## Project objective

Turn the reviewed ONECALL product rules and target architecture into production-ready, testable increments without treating the existing HTML prototypes as production code. Each work unit follows the Gateflow order independently and must preserve the module ownership, trust boundaries, state machines and deferred production decisions in the design source.

## Execution order

The architecture design defines the authoritative coarse order:

1. Phase A: platform foundation.
2. Phase B: IVR task, trial and evidence closure.
3. Phase C: customer purchase and recording sharing.
4. Phase D: AI, notary and production readiness.

Before production implementation, section 18 of the design source requires code-ready specifications in this order: data model and initial schema, OpenAPI surfaces, IVR protocol, security design, capacity/deployment sizing, and the production acceptance matrix. These are delivered as bounded work units rather than one oversized implementation phase.

### PA4 binding goal contract

- Authorization basis: after PR #11 merged, the controller verified `origin/main` and isolated PA4 branch `codex/pa4-v004-catalog-order-payment-foundation` at merge commit `81022037dc5dced625788477835472a84a16d52d`. The user explicitly confirmed the proposed V004-only goal.
- Goal: implement exactly `V004__catalog_order_and_payment.sql` so later consent, entitlement, customer-purchase and payment-processing work has stable catalog product/version/offer/benefit, immutable order snapshot, payment-order and append-only provider-event parents without implementing those application services or any V005+ domain.
- Success signals: Flyway applies V001 through V004 to a clean PostgreSQL 16 database; V004 creates the four frozen S2 schemas and eight NOLOGIN owner/runtime roles, then exactly seven `catalog_order` tables with 78 columns, 51 named checks, seven PKs plus 11 ordinary/partial unique keys, 28 total indexes, 11 V004 ordinary immediate RESTRICT FKs, five local `SECURITY INVOKER` trigger functions and seven triggers. `customer_order.purchase_consent_id` is NOT NULL but has no FK until V007. Exact owner/runtime/cross-module grants and zero-PUBLIC schema/table/function/column privileges are present; V005+ tables and `issue_first_trial` are absent. Fail-closed PostgreSQL 16 evidence covers inventory, lifecycle/revision and immutable-snapshot guards, append-only rows, active-version and identity uniqueness, V004 FK backstops, exact least privilege and bounded catalog publication/order/payment concurrency backstops. Existing default verification and the cumulative container profile pass with no skip/failure/error.
- Immutable prerequisites: V001 SHA-256 `946A0A13E07A4BFE583587A8CF5B1A24C56E3DCFB8CFECA2E28E84427CF81045`, V002 `534A0A06C9520856EF555275100E8B8A194DE80556B336F6ED4C984C3D9795D5` and V003 `1D3FFB5FFD60D1BE5F85B93B87F52C727E7B43DAD5929280123CC567AB873FB1` must remain byte-identical at their Git blobs.
- Scope boundary: the V004 migration, focused cumulative additions to `PostgresContainerIT`, the minimum CI expected-set and `backend/README.md` evidence updates, and required Gateflow artifacts/controller records. Database tests may use bounded transient SQL to prove documented transaction/concurrency backstops but must label all service orchestration as deferred.
- Non-goals: no V005-V009 migration or object; no IVR, entitlement, activation-consent, evidence or sharing table/function; no order-creation, catalog-publication, payment-callback, entitlement-issuance or provider application service; no API, controller, JPA aggregate/repository, frontend, refund/invoice/work-order workflow, runtime DataSource/Flyway wiring, Tencent Cloud resource or production provisioning. Do not add the V007 purchase-consent FK early, invent offer deletion/withdrawal APIs, implement payment-provider integration, or guess Q2/Q17/Q20/Q23 inputs.
- Direct evidence: merged `main` contains only V001-V003; the migration directory has no V004; the cumulative PostgreSQL suite has 25 tests and explicitly asserts the absence of `catalog_order`, the other S2 schemas/roles and every V004+ object. The frozen specification provides complete V004 table dictionaries, creation order, grants, guards and service/deferred boundaries, so no new schema design is required.
- Avoided over-design: do not add generic commerce workflow/state-machine infrastructure, service SQL functions, V005 placeholders, extra provider registries, soft-delete columns, entitlement status on the order, speculative refund/invoice fields, microservices or repository-wide refactors. Preserve the modular-monolith and expand-only Flyway model.
- Blocking open questions: none. OQ/Q items above have explicit later owners and do not block this persistence-only work unit.

### PA4 slice principle

Use behaviorally verifiable increments and as few slices as practical, normally no more than three. Each slice must establish an observable V004 contract boundary and preserve a runnable V001-V004 prefix; do not split mechanically by table, file, schema, owner or technical layer. No application-service or V005+ behavior may enter a slice. The plan must justify its slice count against Gateflow cost and explain any count above three.

Accepted V001/V002 migrations and executable evidence are immutable prerequisites.

### PA3 proposed goal contract

- Authorization basis: the user reported PR #10 merged. GitHub confirms PR #10 `MERGED` at `2026-10-27T13:54:57Z` with merge commit `58c13de895cccf44c0101a865b97e19ae2afef20`; the controller fetched `origin/main`, created a new isolated worktree at that exact commit and created branch `codex/pa3-v003-subject-number-and-claim-foundation` with a clean worktree.
- Goal: implement exactly `V003__subject_number_and_claim.sql` so later activation, IVR, catalog/order and evidence waves have stable business-subject, caller-number-binding, append-only claim and current risk-state parents without implementing their application services or any V004+ domain.
- Success signals: Flyway applies V001, V002 and V003 to a clean PostgreSQL 16 database; V003 creates exactly the four frozen tables `subject_number.business_subject`, `subject_number.caller_number_binding`, `subject_number.subject_claim` and `subject_number.subject_risk_state` with 36 columns, 25 named checks, seven ordinary immediate RESTRICT/RESTRICT FKs, seven PK/ordinary-unique constraints and 18 indexes including two partial unique indexes; the three local `SECURITY INVOKER` immutability guards, the append-only claim guard, exact owner/PUBLIC/schema/table/column/function ACL and narrow real-name-verification role boundary are present. Fail-closed PostgreSQL 16 evidence must cover catalog exactness, null-to-personal owner set-once behavior and real-name separation, binding lifecycle/terminality and active-number uniqueness, append-only claim/check/FK behavior, risk open/clear/re-open revision behavior, runtime/cross-module least privilege and representative concurrency/backstop semantics. V001 and V002 remain byte-for-byte unchanged, and default plus container-profile verification pass.
- Scope boundary: the V003 migration, focused evolution of the existing PostgreSQL 16 executable contract suite, and the minimum CI/README/evidence updates required to run and explain that V003 persistence contract. `subject_number_role` and `subject_identity_verification_role` remain separate; business-number binding is not a login identity; subject real-name is independent from account real-name; optional organization context never chooses or transfers enterprise ownership.
- Non-goals: no V004-V009 migration or object; no activation, consent, IVR task/session/call, trial, entitlement, catalog, order, payment, recording, evidence or sharing object; no API, controller, JPA aggregate/repository, login/session/credential/OTP, verification-provider integration, ciphertext decryption, application claim/binding/risk service, runtime DataSource/Flyway wiring, cloud resource or production provisioning. Do not implement or invent enterprise ownership/transfer policy, role-to-claim permission, outbound-call risk blocking, Q2 key rotation, Q17/Q23 provisioning or later application behavior.
- Direct evidence: `docs/14` sections 4.12-4.15 freeze the four table/lifecycle/sensitive-data contracts; section 5.3 freezes all seven V003 FKs; sections 9.6-9.8 define the service-owned claim, binding and risk protocols whose database backstops must not be confused with production services; sections 10.8/10.8.1 freeze the exact grants and four V003 trigger functions; section 11.3 fixes the V003 creation order. The merged backend contains only V001/V002, 18 container methods and an explicit no-V003+ assertion.
- First-principles judgment: V003 is the smallest useful next migration wave because activation, IVR, orders and evidence all need a stable subject and canonical-number relation, while claims and current risk state must share that same identity boundary. Delivering only the frozen persistence backstops avoids coupling schema work to unresolved enterprise authorization, provider verification and future IVR/risk policy.
- Avoided over-design: do not add service SQL functions, generic workflow/state-machine frameworks, new replay/fingerprint columns, enterprise-owner APIs, credential/session tables, speculative risk policy, V004 placeholders or repository-wide refactors. Use transient test SQL only where necessary to prove the documented database contract, and label service-owned orchestration accurately.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning if the user accepts this boundary. Enterprise ownership/transfer and exact role-to-claim permission remain Product/Subject-number/Organization-access follow-ups; Q24 outbound-call risk policy remains Product/Risk/IVR; Q2/Q17/Q23 remain Security/Platform/Database. They must not be guessed in PA3.

### PA3 slice principle

Use behaviorally verifiable increments and as few slices as practical, normally no more than three. Each slice must establish an observable V003 contract boundary and preserve a runnable V001+V002+V003 prefix; do not split mechanically by table, schema, file or technical layer. No application-service or V004+ behavior may enter a slice.

- ID: `PA2-v002-identity-organization-employee-foundation`
- Type: architecture-sensitive migration and persistence feature work unit.
- Status: completed (`final closeout pass`).

- Current gate / next entry point: `final closeout pass`. Accepted PR-review checkpoint `5cb5c5b0dbdc7252af1d1c25c8da0feebea8ca27` was pushed and matched local HEAD, the remote branch and Draft PR #10; GitHub Actions run `40512932592` passed both required jobs on that exact head. After the user merges PR #10, update local `main` from `origin/main` and start fresh Phaseflow goal confirmation for tentative work unit `PA3-v003-subject-number-and-claim-foundation` from the merged base.
- Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md` sections 5, 6, 14, 15 Phase A and 18 item 1; `docs/14-production-data-model-and-flyway-schema-spec.md` sections 4.6-4.11, 5.3, 9.4, 9.5, 9.9, 9.10, 10.8, 10.8.1 and 11.3 are binding. The accepted PA1 V001 migration and executable evidence remain immutable prerequisites.

### PA2 binding goal contract

- Authorization basis: the user explicitly confirmed the proposed `PA2-v002-identity-organization-employee-foundation` goal after PR #9 merged and the controller verified the isolated PA2 worktree at merged `origin/main` commit `d93c380b9f0025675931f950d95ca4cfef45647b`.
- Goal: implement exactly `V002__identity_organization_and_employee.sql` so later subject, IVR, order and evidence waves have stable customer-account, authentication-identity, account-real-name, organization-membership and operations-employee parents without implementing application authentication or later domain behavior.
- Success signals: Flyway applies V001 then V002 to a clean PostgreSQL 16 database; V002 creates exactly the six frozen tables `customer_iam.customer_account`, `customer_iam.auth_identity`, `customer_iam.identity_profile`, `organization_access.organization`, `organization_access.organization_membership` and `operations_iam.employee_account` with their named checks, keys, indexes and four same-wave immediate FKs; all six local immutability guards and the exact narrow owned/cross-module grants are present; fail-closed PostgreSQL 16 evidence covers inventory, lifecycle/revision behavior, immutable relations, identity collision semantics, membership revoke-and-regrant semantics, least privilege and last-enabled-admin transaction protection. V001 remains byte-for-byte unchanged at SHA-256 `946A0A13E07A4BFE583587A8CF5B1A24C56E3DCFB8CFECA2E28E84427CF81045`, and default plus container-profile verification pass.
- Scope boundary: the V002 migration, focused additions to the existing PostgreSQL 16 executable contract suite and the minimum CI/README/evidence updates required to run and explain that V002 contract. Customer and employee authentication domains stay separate; membership is authorization after customer login, never an authentication identity; account-level real-name remains separate from subject-level real-name.
- Non-goals: no V003-V009 migration or object; no subject, phone-number, claim, risk, activation, consent, IVR, trial, catalog, order, payment, evidence or sharing behavior; no API, login/session/credential/OTP implementation, frontend, runtime DataSource/Flyway wiring, cloud resource, production provisioning or domain workflow. Do not guess Q2 key-rotation behavior or Q17/Q23 production role/provisioning inputs.
- Direct evidence: `docs/14` sections 4.6-4.11 freeze the six table contracts and lifecycle rules; section 5.3 freezes the four V002 FKs; sections 9.4/9.5/9.9/9.10 define login projection, identity collision, last-admin and lifecycle semantics; sections 10.8/10.8.1 freeze least-privilege grants and six guard triggers; section 11.3 fixes V002 creation order. The merged backend contains only V001 plus its 12-method PostgreSQL evidence and explicitly states that V002+ is absent.
- First-principles judgment: V002 is the smallest useful next migration wave because all later subject, task, order and evidence facts need these stable parent identities, while implementing only the frozen persistence contract avoids coupling schema delivery to still-deferred authentication/session and domain-service designs.
- Avoided over-design: do not introduce JPA aggregates, repositories, service endpoints, database service functions, generic trigger frameworks, credential/session tables, speculative key rotation or later-wave compatibility objects. Add only abstractions that are necessary to express and verify the frozen V002 contract in the existing test structure.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning. Q2 provider-subject rotation and Q17/Q23 production security/provisioning remain explicitly deferred and cannot be guessed.

### PA2 slice principle

Use behaviorally verifiable increments and as few slices as practical, normally no more than three. Each slice must establish an observable V002 contract boundary and preserve a runnable V001+V002 prefix; do not split mechanically by table, schema, file or technical layer. No V003+ or application authentication behavior may enter a slice.

### PA1 binding goal contract

- Authorization basis: the user confirmed the proposed `PA1-v001-persistence-foundation` goal after PR #8 merged and the controller synchronized local `main` to `9fe3619af968d896400742d90df819bb66d868bd`.
- Goal: implement the accepted A0 V001 PostgreSQL persistence foundation required by subsequent modules, without implementing any later migration wave or product domain behavior.
- Success signals: Flyway applies V001 to a clean PostgreSQL 16 Testcontainers database without skipping; exactly the V001 schemas, roles, three tables, append-only audit guard and four declared SQL functions/grants are present; tests prove idempotency `NEW/REPLAY/CONFLICT` and finalize-once behavior, Outbox same-key replay/conflict and transactional rollback, audit append-only behavior, fixed `search_path`, owner/`PUBLIC` execution boundaries, and migration failure is visible rather than silently skipped. Default verification and the container profile both pass.
- Scope boundary: backend persistence foundation for only `V001__platform_schemas_roles_idempotency_outbox_audit.sql`, its Flyway/PostgreSQL runtime wiring, and focused PostgreSQL 16 tests of the accepted V001 contract. V001 may create the empty later-domain schemas and NOLOGIN roles explicitly required by the frozen migration order, but no V002+ tables or domain behavior.
- Non-goals: no V002-V009 migrations; no customer/employee identity, organization, subject, number, activation, consent, IVR, trial, catalog, order, payment, evidence, sharing, or operations workflows; no API, UI, cloud resource, secrets, production role provisioning, queue/dispatcher, or guessed Q17/Q23 deployment values. No changes to A0's accepted table/function contracts or later-wave behavior.
- Direct evidence: the accepted A0 spec defines the exact V001 wave, tables, four functions, grants, fixed search paths, denylist and V001 verification requirements. The backend currently contains only the Boot 4.1.2 / Modulith 2.1.2 runtime scaffold and architecture/container tests; `backend/README.md` states it has no domain behavior, table or migration, and `backend/pom.xml` currently excludes Flyway and JDBC dependencies.
- First-principles judgment: this is the smallest useful next persistence increment because V001 supplies the transaction-safe idempotency, Outbox and audit primitives needed by every later domain wave; implementing those frozen primitives before identity/domain tables avoids duplicating reliability behavior. The work is constrained to the already accepted V001 contract.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning. Q17/Q23 production migration-role and `SECURITY DEFINER` deployment provisioning remain explicit deferred inputs; they are not to be guessed and do not block local Testcontainers verification.

### PA1 slice principle

Keep implementation slices behaviorally verifiable and as few as practical, normally no more than three. A slice must establish and test an observable V001 contract boundary; do not split mechanically by file, module or technology. No V002+ work may be pulled into a slice.

### PA1 plan review adjudication

- **F-PA1-PR-01 — accepted, mandatory implementation clarification (S1):** the broken-migration proof must use an isolated Flyway history schema/table and a non-colliding test migration version; it must assert that the first valid DDL actually executed and is absent after rollback. A mere `FlywayException` caused by shared history/version collision is not acceptance evidence. Owner/destination: Backend, S1 implementation and code review.
- **F-PA1-PR-02 — accepted, mandatory evidence (S1):** keep `BootstrapDependencyBoundaryTests` unchanged and record a negative surefire-classpath probe in the implementation completion report, in addition to the Enforcer scope probe and fat-jar check. Owner/destination: Backend/Platform, S1 completion report.
- **F-PA1-PR-03 — accepted-risk:** keep the shared container but scope all row counts to method-local keys, exclude Flyway/system metadata from inventory, and clean temporary schemas/roles in `finally` paths. Owner/destination: Backend/QA, S2/S3 implementation and review.
- **Slice-CI clarification — accepted:** the final plan's 12-test CI assertion is a final-state target, not an S1 prerequisite. Each accepted slice must keep CI green by asserting exactly the container tests implemented at that checkpoint; S1 asserts only its migration/inventory/failure tests, S2 expands the assertion with its reliability tests, and S3 reaches the final 12 named tests. Do not add skipped/placeholder tests or weaken fail-closed checks to make counts match. Owner/destination: Platform/CI, each slice.
- **R-15/OQ-PR-01 — accepted/rejected as resolved:** `docs/14` §2.1 is binding and requires each module runtime role to receive `USAGE` on its own schema without `CREATE`; the plan must preserve this grant even where §10.8 focuses on table/function grants. This is not a new privilege beyond the frozen contract.
- **OQ-PR-02 — deferred-with-owner:** the implementation completion artifact filename is fixed at implementation intake by the controller; this does not block the accepted plan checkpoint. Owner/destination: Phaseflow, PA1 implementation gate.

The review found no goal drift, V002+ leakage, production-provisioning guess, or structural slice defect. The plan is accepted as the binding implementation handoff only with the above S1/S2/S3 obligations; implementation agents may not invent a different failure-test isolation or privilege boundary.

### PA0 binding goal contract

- Authorization basis: after PR #7 was verified merged, the controller inspected the repository/runtime gap, restated the PA0 goal, success signals, non-goals, exact scope boundary, direct evidence and avoided over-design. The user replied `确认` on 2026-10-26. On 2026-10-27, after the accepted Boot 3.5 plan failed closed on Spring's OSS support window, the user explicitly selected the migration path and fixed the new target line to Boot 4.1.x + Spring Modulith 2.1.x; Boot 4.0.x is explicitly excluded.
- Goal: create the first bounded production platform foundation so later Phase A domain work has a repeatable Java runtime, enforceable module boundaries, supported toolchains and a minimal CI/Testcontainers path.
- Success signals: establish Java 21, Maven and a GA-compatible Spring Boot 4.1.x/Spring Modulith 2.1.x scaffold; make module-boundary verification executable and non-trivial; provide one minimal bootable/testable application entry without domain state machines; provide a PostgreSQL Testcontainers smoke path that may be explicitly absent locally but cannot silently skip in CI; pin Java/Maven/Node 22; and run the Java checks plus existing A1-A5 regressions in a minimal GitHub CI workflow. CI remains build/static/synthetic evidence, never production acceptance. Boot 4.0.x is not an acceptable fallback.
- Scope boundary: repository/runtime scaffolding, package/module dependency enforcement, supported toolchain pinning and minimum repeatable CI/Testcontainers smoke only. Exact patch versions must be backed by authoritative compatibility evidence and may not be guessed.
- Non-goals: no A0 44-table schema or V001-V009 migrations; no identity, activation, commerce, entitlement, IVR, evidence, sharing, AI, notary, refund, invoice or support behavior; no IVR/WeChat/COS/KMS/Redis/RabbitMQ/production PostgreSQL/Tencent Cloud integration; no full SBOM/image/Kubernetes/deployment/performance/release pipeline; no invented credentials, capacity, retention, SLO/RPO/RTO, region, SKU, cloud capability or acceptance-authority value.
- Direct evidence: the repository has no tracked Java/Maven/Gradle project, wrapper, Dockerfile/compose or GitHub workflow. `docs/12` requires Java 21, a Spring Boot/Spring Modulith runtime, Maven, module tests, PostgreSQL Testcontainers and CI; A0 explicitly delivered no executable migration or Maven project; A5 assigned Node 22 and repository CI to Platform/CI. The prior Boot 3.5.16 + Modulith 1.4.14 preflight resolved technically but was blocked because Spring's official API reports the 3.5.x OSS support end date as 2026-06-30. The controller environment has Temurin 21 available for diagnostics, Node 22.23.4, Docker client/server 29.1.3 and no PA0 container smoke executed yet.
- Blocking open questions at revised goal confirmation: the exact GA patch versions within Boot 4.1.x and Modulith 2.1.x, their compatibility, support status and dependency pins must be established by the revised plan's authoritative preflight. The plan may not silently substitute Boot 4.0.x or an unsupported patch.

### PA0 slice principle

Use the fewest behaviorally verifiable increments, normally at most three. Slice by independently testable behavior/evidence boundaries rather than file, module, technology layer or owner. Do not let runtime scaffolding absorb later Phase A domains, migrations, provider integrations or release hardening.

## Previous work unit: A5

- ID: `A5-production-acceptance-matrix-spec`
- Type: architecture-sensitive production acceptance specification deliverable.
- Status: completed (`final closeout pass`).
- Base: merged `origin/main` commit `9b54f0fc29946123f1fb6ee80b5845c51c080d65` (PR #6 / A4); local `main` was fast-forwarded and this dedicated A5 branch was created from the same commit while preserving unrelated screenshot changes and `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md`.
- Current gate / next entry point: `final closeout pass`. Accepted PR-review checkpoint `b18d9956c91df0779efc325df201c1f8969f5416` was followed by controller bookkeeping HEAD `0d1ab9963943c2876dd0eb09d16dfe392c5898d0`; the reviewed-content push was verified at that HEAD for both the remote branch and Draft PR #7 before the controller-only closeout delivery commit. Final closeout is recorded in `docs/gateflow/a5-production-acceptance-matrix-spec-final-closeout.md`. After the user merges PR #7, update `main` from `origin/main` and start fresh Phaseflow goal confirmation for the first bounded Phase A platform-foundation implementation work unit, tentatively `PA0-production-runtime-bootstrap`.
- Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md` sections 12-14 and 18 item 6; accepted A0/A1/A2/A3/A4 specifications, models, validators and residual-risk destinations remain binding inputs.

### A5 binding goal contract

- Authorization basis: after PR #6 was merged, the controller verified GitHub state `MERGED` at `9b54f0fc29946123f1fb6ee80b5845c51c080d65`, fast-forwarded local `main`, created the dedicated A5 branch, and restated the goal, success signal, non-goals and boundary. The user replied `确认` on 2026-10-26.
- Goal: deliver a code-generation-ready, statically verifiable production acceptance matrix that maps every critical accepted state machine and cross-system convergence path to normal, duplicate, timeout, out-of-order, recovery and permission-denied scenarios without redefining the underlying product, API, protocol, security or capacity contracts.
- Success signal: each scenario has a stable ID and source-contract references; names the owning aggregate/service and execution tier; defines preconditions, stimulus, ordering/fault injection, expected persisted state and terminal absorption, idempotency/replay behavior, zero or bounded side effects, audit/Outbox/metric/log evidence, cleanup/recovery expectations, evidence owner and acceptance result semantics. Each of the six required scenario axes is represented for every critical family or explicitly marked not applicable with a contract-backed reason; silence is not coverage.
- Critical families: customer/employee identity and workspace access; subject/number/risk lifecycle; activation/consent/first trial; catalog/order/payment/entitlement/usage reservation; IVR task/decision/session/call/leg/outbox/reconciliation; recording/media/evidence; share grant/admission/ticket/playback; authentication/signing/replay, credentials, audit/high-risk mutation and redaction; deployment dependencies, failover, restore and capacity gates where A3/A4 require runtime evidence.
- Scope boundary: documentation plus a minimal dependency-free deterministic model/validator and focused synthetic fixtures only where needed to prove matrix completeness, source-reference integrity, negative/zero-side-effect assertions and honest evidence states. Reuse accepted A0-A4 identifiers and registries instead of copying whole contracts into a new truth source. Existing HTML prototypes remain demonstration/QA assets, not production acceptance evidence.
- Non-goals: no production Java/frontend/IVR/provider implementation; no live carrier, SIP/CDR, cloud, database, queue, payment, KMS/COS, penetration, load or restore execution; no CI platform build-out; no invented timeout/SLO/RPO/RTO/retention/concurrency/price/Region/AZ/SKU or policy value; no production-pass claim from static or synthetic checks. AI, notary, refund, invoice, online work-order, PBX and other workflows without accepted upstream production contracts are not invented; they remain deferred with owners/destinations.
- Acceptance honesty: matrix coverage, model validation and synthetic fixtures prove specification completeness only. A scenario requiring runtime/cloud/line/manual evidence stays `NOT_RUN` or equivalent evidence-missing state until an authorized execution supplies immutable evidence; no aggregate production-acceptance flag may become true from caller assertion, synthetic fixture success or inherited design completeness.
- Direct evidence: architecture §18 item 6 explicitly requires mapping every critical state machine to the six scenario axes; A0 already defines 44 tables, 40 invariants, lifecycle machines and behavior matrices; A1 freezes five trust surfaces and stable Problem mappings; A2 freezes task/call/reservation/media replay, conflict and recovery machines while declaring its evaluator is not a production acceptance matrix; A3/A4 likewise declare their fixtures are not runtime/cloud/capacity acceptance. The work unit closes this cross-specification coverage/evidence gap without implementing the systems under test.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning. Missing production values and executable environments are typed evidence dependencies, not permission to invent them and not blockers for the static acceptance specification.

### A5 slice principle

Use the fewest behaviorally verifiable increments, preferably at most three. Slice by coherent acceptance outcome and executable evidence boundary, not by source document, subsystem, scenario-axis column or team owner. Avoid a giant duplicate registry of A0-A4 and avoid one fixture per matrix cell when a focused adversarial fixture can prove a general invariant. Any plan exceeding three slices must prove why the gate cost is justified.

### A4 binding goal contract

- Authorization basis: the user reported PR #5 merged; the controller verified GitHub merge state and commit, then clarified that no numeric line concurrency had been approved. The user replied `了解了 你继续任务` on 2026-10-26. This authorizes continuing A4 with explicitly deferred numeric inputs; it does not approve any concurrency, retention, region, SKU, price, SLO or recovery target. The controller restated the parameterized scope and success signal before dispatch.
- Goal: deliver a parameterized, reviewable capacity/deployment specification for the approved Tencent Cloud + private COS + existing carrier-room IVR architecture, ready to receive real workload measurements and approved operating policies.
- Success signal: calculations identify their units, input provenance/approval status, formulas, missing prerequisites and limitations; cover IVR call legs versus complete bridged calls, business API load, recording/upload/playback bandwidth, encrypted local spool, stored media/versions/derived data, metadata/audit/queue growth, measured node capacity/failover, and backup/recovery dependencies. Missing production inputs produce an explicit incomplete/deferred result, never a procurement-ready recommendation. Small synthetic examples may test arithmetic but are never approved production values.
- Deployment outcome: describe placement, trust/network boundaries, the existing media-gateway data path, release/rollback sequence, scaling and validation gates, backup/restore measurements and a capability-check checklist. Define the evidence required to select actual Tencent region/AZ/SKU and verify KMS/Secret Manager/CAM/COS capabilities without claiming those checks already passed.
- Scope boundary: documentation plus a small dependency-free, deterministic calculation/validation aid and focused fixtures only if useful to verify the sizing formulas. No parallel registry of all A0/A1/A2/A3 contracts, large policy engine, or count-only success assertions. Preserve their ownership, public API/protocol/security semantics and named residual owners/destinations.
- Non-goals: no cloud procurement/provisioning, live capability verification, production service/Java/UI implementation, migration or public contract changes, IVR replacement, microservice split, Kubernetes, legal-retention decisions, invented production defaults or a claim of production sizing/readiness. Numeric missing inputs are not silently substituted from prototypes or synthetic tests.
- Open inputs: complete-call concurrency and inbound/outbound/shared line limits; arrival rates and call/ringing durations; real recording format/bitrate/channels; daily volumes and playback/AI load; media/metadata/audit/backup retention; outage-buffer target; measured per-node throughput and resource demand; cloud region/AZ/product capability, unit prices and budget; SLO/RPO/RTO. Owners remain Telephony/IVR, Product/Operations, Infrastructure/Security, Database/Backend and Legal as applicable. These block final production sizing and procurement, not the authorized parameterized specification.

### A4 slice principle

Use the fewest behaviorally verifiable increments warranted by this specification/calculator scope, preferably at most three. Do not mechanically split by file, cloud product or owner. Avoid multiplying gate/report/model scaffolding beyond what is needed to prove dimensional correctness, honest incomplete results and deployment/recovery alignment.

### A4 plan-review adjudication

- F-01 accepted: S1 template/validation covers its seven groups; S2 adds two groups and may update the template; test API/node behavior in the slice that implements it. Assertions prove behavior, not a prescribed fixture count.
- F-02 accepted: use measured gross recovery upload throughput and subtract contemporaneous continuing production once. Zero backlog drains in zero time; positive backlog with nonpositive net throughput cannot drain. Clarify disk capacity/free-space accounting.
- F-03 accepted: retain headroom in N-1 arithmetic; restrict linear node multiplication to qualified homogeneous stateless pools. Stateful stores require measured normal/degraded topology capacity; resource evidence cannot be merely echoed and then certify fit. Logical media-gateway shares existing core-api/edge resources.
- F-04 accepted: explicit typed recovery target/drill fields, valid zero RPO, deterministic `asOf`, distinct missing/excluded outcomes, and target-versus-observation comparisons. Evidence completeness never proves actual recovery.
- F-05 accepted: explicit measured/derived upload average and peak contracts; codec rate alone is not file-upload speed. Separate payload/wire overhead and catch-up traffic.
- F-06 accepted: metric-level partial outputs; independent derived retention/version inputs; total all-channel bitrate; legal-hold bytes are incremental outside ordinary totals.
- F-07 accepted: capability data are caller assertions with provenance/owner/reference/date; no automatic cloud verification or A3 risk closure.
- F-08 accepted: strict-JSON negatives plus programmatic true JS nonfinite-number probes.
- F-09 accepted: valid explicit zero traffic yields zero demand; denominator handling belongs only to actual division.
- Additional directly evidenced contract gaps are bounded corrections to the same success signal: specify per-leaf units/source/owner/approval and collection/output structures; Little's-law means are not guaranteed peak ports; conditional cache math cannot approve a CDN topology; independent AI/task/queue demand and database-overhead limitations remain explicit measured inputs. None selects production values, expands public contracts or adds slices.
- Re-review 2 open questions 1-3 are accepted as bounded S1 implementation interpretations: retention `days` inputs use non-negative `NonNegativeMeasure` semantics with no new integer or upper-bound rule; report the seven S1 template-group/input-ID mapping from the implemented template without creating a parallel registry; `IDLE_ZERO_DENOMINATOR` returns metric value `0`, status `COMPLETE`, and that reason code. S1 implementation report must confirm each interpretation and tests must cover the zero-denominator behavior.
- Re-review 2 conclusion is `pass-with-risks`; these interpretations and the production inputs/inherited cloud risks remain explicitly tracked and do not authorize production sizing, procurement, cloud verification, or readiness claims.

### A4 inherited risk handling

- A3 PR #5 was verified MERGED at `f3bcbd99a6f49cb64977358cea08e028bc634a54`; its historical closeout below and in its own artifact remains an immutable account of pre-merge facts.
- All A3 named residuals remain open with their original owners/destinations until actual evidence closes them. A4 directly maps `R-A3-COS-CAPABILITY`, `R-A3-NO-WORM`, `R-A3-REGION-SKU`, `R-A3-MEDIA-GATEWAY-TRANSPORT` and `R-A3-AUDIT-VOLUME`; legal retention, key recovery and related integration/security residuals remain explicit dependencies. A static checklist or synthetic calculation cannot close a cloud/runtime risk.
- Repository CI is not configured; independently executed static checks are evidence for this deliverable, not passing production CI, a live cloud test or a performance result.

## Previous work unit: A3

- ID: `A3-production-security-design-spec`
- Type: architecture-sensitive security specification deliverable.
- Status: completed (`final closeout pass`).
- Base: merged `origin/main` commit `a6018f5ebf205dfe9519820c63fddfa43c508256` (PR #4 / A2).
- Current gate / next entry point: `final closeout pass`. Accepted PR-review checkpoint `25f4a0369f731b5f99fcb6a859cd7ee9bcdfcb08` was pushed and verified as both the remote branch and Draft PR #5 head. Final closeout is recorded in `docs/gateflow/a3-production-security-design-spec-final-closeout.md`. After the user merges PR #5, update `main` and start a new Phaseflow goal confirmation for `A4-production-capacity-deployment-spec` from architecture section 18 item 5.
- Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md` sections 4, 11 and 18, interpreted with the accepted A0 data model and security-question contracts in `docs/14-production-data-model-and-flyway-schema-spec.md`, the accepted A1 trust surfaces in `api/openapi/onecall-v1.openapi.yaml` and `docs/15-production-openapi-surface-spec.md`, and the accepted A2 signing/media/recovery boundaries in `docs/16-production-ivr-protocol-spec.md`.

### A3 binding goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready, statically verifiable production security design covering the key hierarchy and purpose separation, envelope encryption and HMAC indexes, key rotation and replay compatibility, customer/employee/IVR/provider authentication domains, service identities and token audiences, IVR/provider callback signing and replay protection, short-lived media credentials, Tencent COS private-object policy, audit access, log redaction and high-risk operation controls.
- Success signal: every sensitive datum, key, credential and service call has an explicit owner, storage location, purpose separation, rotation/recovery path, fail-closed behavior, audit event and testable scenario.
- Cloud boundary: Tencent Cloud China deployment and COS private buckets are fixed. The design maps requirements to Tencent KMS, Secret Manager, CAM and COS capabilities without inventing a region, SKU, price, capacity or unverified WORM/object-lock capability.
- Scope boundary: security specification plus bounded machine-readable validation or fixtures only. Existing accepted A0/A1/A2 contracts remain authoritative and may be refined only when an unavoidable contradiction is proved and separately reviewed.
- Non-goals: no Java/runtime implementation, actual cloud provisioning, real credentials, UI/prototype changes, capacity/deployment sizing, legal retention decision, WeChat/real-name/payment provider implementation, Kubernetes, service mesh, SPIFFE, custom IAM engine or microservice split. Keep the fixed `ADMIN` and `OPS` employee roles.
- Deferred inputs: legal retention, MFA/identity provider, exact Tencent region and verified regional capabilities, production TTL/rate-limit/replay-window values, organization permission matrix and vendor-specific policies remain typed inputs with explicit owners and destinations rather than guessed values.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning.

### A3 project constraints

- Preserve the accepted A0 ciphertext envelope `v1.<key_ref>.<base64url(nonce || authenticated_ciphertext_and_tag)>` and HMAC-SHA-256 lookup shape; the plan must define ownership, associated-data binding, purpose separation, version compatibility and rotation/recovery rather than silently replacing those contracts.
- Preserve the five accepted A1 trust surfaces and their separate sessions, credentials and audiences. Customer, employee, IVR, public-share and provider callback authentication must not collapse into one interchangeable token domain.
- Preserve A2 protocol ownership: IVR owns telephony/media facts while ONECALL owns business authorization, entitlement and evidence state. Signing, timestamp, nonce and replay controls must fail closed without causing redial, double settlement or contradictory event acceptance.
- Media upload and playback authority must be short-lived, least-privilege, object/purpose scoped and independently auditable. COS remains private; permanent object URLs, long-lived client credentials and raw object keys must not cross public contracts.
- Secret material, plaintext sensitive values, credential-bearing URLs and authentication artifacts must be excluded or deterministically redacted from logs, traces, errors, analytics and ordinary support views.
- Security controls must map to the existing modular-monolith and self-built-IVR architecture. The plan must not create new infrastructure topology or organizational roles merely to describe a control.
- Existing HTML prototypes, `shared/onecall-business.js`, modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside A3 and must remain untouched and unstaged.

### A3 slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and minimize Gateflow review cost. Group the specification by coherent security outcomes and adversarial failure behavior, not mechanically by document section, cloud product, trust surface or owner. Prefer no more than three implementation slices; a plan proposing more must prove why they cannot be safely combined without weakening validation or mixing incompatible security invariants.

## Completed work unit: A2 production IVR protocol specification

- ID: `A2-production-ivr-protocol-spec`
- Type: architecture-sensitive protocol specification deliverable.
- Status: completed (`final closeout pass`).
- Base: merged `origin/main` commit `754b7dfc53c277e79c5d63e24d578a93fa557cda` (PR #2 / A1).
- Current gate / next entry point: `final closeout pass`. PR review checkpoint `2ccec09762d0fff754cb89968da838480fa69969` was pushed and verified as both the remote branch and Draft PR #4 head. Final closeout is recorded in `docs/gateflow/a2-production-ivr-protocol-spec-final-closeout.md`. After the user merges PR #4, update `main` and start a new Phaseflow goal confirmation for `A3-production-security-design-spec` from architecture section 18 item 4.
- Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md` sections 8 and 18, interpreted with `docs/13-ivr-call-task-and-inbound-design.md`, the accepted A0 schema specification `docs/14-production-data-model-and-flyway-schema-spec.md`, the accepted A1 API contract `api/openapi/onecall-v1.openapi.yaml`, and `docs/15-production-openapi-surface-spec.md`.
- Motivation and direct evidence: A1 deliberately leaves the detailed IVR session, event, retry, recovery and media protocol to A2/Q15/Q26/Q28. The accepted API surface exposes resolve, activation confirmation, outbound authorization, call event, recording declaration and recording completion operations, while the existing IVR design already fixes one nonterminal task per normalized caller, a configured-and-frozen 30-second default task TTL, atomic claim/reservation/frozen action, task-path automatic B-leg dialing, direct-path DTMF flow and IVR ownership of telephony/media facts. A2 must close the deterministic wire and recovery contract before runtime implementation.

### A2 binding goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready, versioned IVR protocol specification that tells the existing self-built IVR and ONECALL backend exactly how to coordinate from inbound session creation through unified action execution, activation re-decision, B-leg recovery, bridge/recording events, heartbeat, terminal settlement and recording media upload.
- Success signal: every protocol message has a stable identity, owner and state precondition; initial `resolve` and post-activation re-decision share one action-selection contract; normal, duplicate, delayed, reordered, conflicting and lost-response paths are explicit; an IVR B-leg operation is exactly-once from the IVR perspective; terminal, recording and media states map exactly to A0 and A1; unknown outcomes fail closed and remain recoverable without redial or double charging; protocol compatibility, stable errors and deterministic static validation or executable fixtures are defined.
- Scope boundary: protocol specification and deterministic static validation/fixtures only. The work unit may add the canonical IVR protocol specification, bounded machine-readable fixtures or validators, Gateflow artifacts and controller updates. A1 schemas may be refined only if an unavoidable contradiction is proven and separately reviewed; additive protocol detail should otherwise map to the accepted A1 surface.
- Non-goals: no Java/runtime service implementation, IVR media-engine rewrite, SIP/provider-specific telephony design, Tencent Cloud resources, actual credentials, production numeric security values, UI/prototype changes, payment/order flows, PBX/extensions/seat PIN, operator-system integration, capacity/deployment sizing or production acceptance matrix.
- No over-design: specify only the lifecycle, sequencing, identity, idempotency, recovery, compatibility and error rules required by the approved IVR behavior. Keep security key hierarchy, numeric replay windows, duration tolerances, authorization windows and provider details as typed deferred inputs instead of inventing Q7/Q8/Q10/Q18/Q25/Q27 values.
- Blocking open questions: none for planning. Q15/Q26/Q28 protocol shapes and recovery semantics are the work unit's target; unresolved numeric, provider and policy values remain named deferred inputs and cannot be guessed by the plan.

### A2 project constraints

- The self-built IVR already owns inbound answering, outbound B-leg dialing, bridging, recording, timed hangup, call facts and media upload. The ONECALL backend owns activation, outbound tasks, entitlement, reservation/settlement and evidence state; operator systems do not participate.
- One normalized caller may have only one nonterminal outbound task. Task creation replaces the prior pending task; default expiry is 30 seconds, server-configured and frozen on that task. Resolve atomically claims the task, reserves entitlement and returns the frozen action. The IVR executes that action and must not independently recompute routing or select a different callee.
- The task path automatically dials the frozen callee after one successful inbound claim; it has no callee confirmation in the voice call. The no-task direct path may collect and confirm a callee through DTMF under its separate authorization flow.
- The same `callId` roots the inbound session, task claim, B-leg operation, legs, bridge, quota reservation, terminal facts, recording, media object and evidence chain. Stable event/operation identities must distinguish exact replay from semantic conflict.
- Initial resolve and activation confirmation must converge on one shared action-selection result shape and invariant set. Activation confirmation cannot silently create a separate routing policy or bypass the original call/session boundary.
- Lost backend responses, delayed/reordered callbacks and IVR restart/recovery must not cause a second B-leg dial, a second reservation/settlement or contradictory terminal/media facts. Unknown telephony outcomes fail closed until authoritative reconciliation.
- A0 persistence ownership and state machines and A1 paths/schemas/errors remain authoritative. The protocol must not expose COS object keys, permanent media URLs, credentials, raw security material or provider internals.
- Existing HTML prototypes, `shared/onecall-business.js`, modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside A2 and must remain untouched and unstaged.

### A2 slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and minimize Gateflow review cost. Group protocol work by coherent end-to-end behavior and failure recovery, not mechanically by files, message types or IVR/backend ownership. Prefer no more than three implementation slices; a plan proposing more must prove why they cannot be safely combined without weakening validation or mixing incompatible state transitions.

- Motivation and direct evidence: the architecture requires OpenAPI contracts for customer, operations, IVR, public sharing and callbacks immediately after the data-model specification. The repository has no production Maven/Spring project, existing OpenAPI/Swagger source or generated client; its HTML prototypes and QA fixtures are behavior evidence only.

### A1 binding goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready, machine-readable OpenAPI 3.1 contract source and the minimum companion mapping/validation artifacts for the five approved trust surfaces: customer (`/api/v1/customer/**`), operations (`/api/v1/operations/**`), self-built IVR (`/api/v1/ivr/**`), public recording shares (`/api/v1/public/shares/**`) and authenticated provider callbacks (`/api/v1/callbacks/**`).
- Success signal: every approved first-release operation has an explicit owner surface, authentication/trust requirement, server-derived authorization boundary, request/response schema, stable status and RFC 9457 error mapping, idempotency or event identity semantics for writes, cursor semantics where a list is exposed, and direct mapping to the accepted A0 data/state contracts. Generated TypeScript-client compatibility and OpenAPI validation are specified and reproducible without claiming a runtime service exists.
- Scope boundary: document/API-contract design and static validation only. The work unit may add canonical OpenAPI source, bounded explanatory or validation artifacts, Gateflow artifacts and controller updates. The interface contracts must not contradict the A0 schema or approved customer/IVR/share flows.
- Non-goals: no backend/Maven project, executable controller/service code, Flyway migration, UI/prototype change, payment or identity-provider integration, cloud resource, vendor credential, provider-specific callback payload, detailed IVR session/retry/media protocol, security numeric policy (token TTL, replay window, KDF/rate limits), organization-role matrix, online work-order/refund/invoice workflow or microservice split.
- No over-design: use REST/JSON, OpenAPI 3.1 and RFC 9457 as required by the architecture; model only approved first-release verbs and state transitions. Keep unresolved values and exact provider/policy formats as explicit typed inputs or later-work-unit destinations instead of inventing security values, vendors, configurations or generic workflow endpoints.
- Blocking open questions: none for the surface contract. Q1/Q6/Q7/Q8/Q10/Q12/Q14/Q15/Q18/Q19/Q22/Q25/Q26/Q27/Q28 remain named, owned inputs. A1 may define safe interface boundaries and stable business errors around them, but must not select their unapproved values or detailed protocols.

### A1 project constraints

- Customer, employee, IVR service, public capability and callback identities stay separate; client input never establishes account, workspace, role, price, payment success, evidence solidification or recording ownership.
- IVR inbound routing must preserve the one-normalized-caller/one-nonterminal-task invariant, 30-second configured-and-frozen task expiry baseline, atomic claim/reservation/frozen authorization, no-task direct-trial flow and no implicit latest-callee fallback.
- Sharing must preserve verification modes `NONE`, code, designated mobile and enterprise member; page open/prefetch cannot consume admission, while atomic admission/replay/revocation generation remain server enforced.
- Operations keeps only fixed `ADMIN` and `OPS` roles; employee authentication source and MFA are deferred. Operations metadata access does not imply recording playback.
- Payment remains customer mini-program/Web initiated and server-confirmed; IVR only gives purchase guidance. Work orders, refunds and invoices remain offline support entries, not online workflows.
- A0 supplies the accepted persistence names, immutable facts, state machines and concurrency/ownership constraints. An API projection cannot expose COS keys, raw credentials, protected personal data, internal SQL structures or internal/provider failure detail.
- Existing HTML prototypes, `shared/onecall-business.js`, modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside A1 and must remain untouched and unstaged.

### A1 slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and minimize Gateflow review cost. The planning Agent must group the canonical contract and explanatory validation by cohesive external behavior rather than mechanically by files or API namespaces; a plan proposing more than three slices must explain why they cannot be safely combined.

#### A1 S3 code-review result and finding adjudication

- Fresh reviewer task `a1-openapi-s3-review-ds-20261024-01` used Claude Code / `ds` / actual model `deepseek-flash[1m]`, exited successfully and created only `docs/reviews/code-review-20261024-181047.md`. Its independent conclusion is fail with eleven findings; its inability to execute the dynamic suite is not treated as controller validation evidence.
- Controller accepts F-01 through F-11 after direct reconciliation against the accepted A1 plan, A0 tables/transactions and docs/13. The fixes remain within the approved five-surface static contract; no runtime implementation, new operation, provider choice, security numeric policy or UI work is authorized.
- F-01 resolution is explicit: public/IVR `taskId`, `callId`, `recordingId` and `shareId` expose the A0 protocol identities (`outbound_call_task.task_id`, `call_record.call_id`/`inbound_session.call_id`, `recording.recording_id`, `evidence_share_grant.share_id`) with accepted bounds 1-64/1-128/1-128/1-128. They do not expose internal UUID primary keys. The contradictory A0 §10.14 `task.taskId = uuid` projection line is classified as an A0 documentation residual owned by A0/IVR contract maintenance; it does not override the accepted A1 §4.10 identity rule or A0 table definitions.
- F-03 correction must make first-result and replay payloads distinguishable without reconstructing secrets. C15 201 uses a satisfiable first-creation schema that requires `capabilityToken` and conditionally carries `extractionCode` only for `EXTRACTION_CODE`; C15 200 uses metadata-only `ShareState`. C18 keeps one operation/status contract but uses a closed discriminated result (`ROTATED` requires the one-time `extractionCode`; `REPLAYED` forbids it), never returns `capabilityToken`, and updates generated compatibility assertions and docs.
- F-06 resolves the source ambiguity in favor of the binding plan: `protocolVersion` is required IVR input, length 1-32, authenticated/fingerprinted rather than server-derived. Event `sequenceNo` is optional with minimum 1, payload is required, `legId` is 1-64 and task `expiresAt` is non-null.
- F-09 is accepted in full because docs/13 §6.4 and A0 §9.14/§10.17 provide sufficient evidence: I2 `decision: ACCEPT` becomes required; I3 carries required `promptVersion`, `confirmationKey` and `confirmationContent` in addition to event/callee so the frozen fingerprint is representable. Exact future allowed-key negotiation remains Q15/Q26 and must not be invented.
- F-11 is accepted because P2/P3 re-check current token/grant/generation state after page open; race outcomes cannot be delegated solely to P1. Their machine-readable inventories, declared responses, exact-set validator and docs must agree. P3 continues to omit `SHARE_LOCKED` because an already admitted valid session remains eligible.

### A1 plan-review finding adjudication

| Finding | Decision | Required correction / destination |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F-01 | accepted | Remove C19/O18 support-entry operations. The approved unified offline contact/instructions remain UI/deployment configuration, not an anonymous persisted-business API or a fabricated A0 mapping. Record that exclusion in the revised plan. |
| F-02 | accepted | Require every `x-onecall-a0-models` entry to be an exact schema-qualified name from the A0 44-table catalog. Expand shorthand/wildcards, including all required `catalog_order.*` entries, and make static validation reject short names, wildcards and wrong schemas. |
| F-03 | accepted | Add a distinct `ShareCodeRotation` response. It may return the new extraction code once but can never return or reconstruct `capabilityToken`; add generated-type validation for that exclusion. |
| F-04 | accepted | Model ordinary frozen IVR outcomes as `200 IvrDecision`, including in-progress/reject/confirmation-not-allowed/consent-required/direct-authorization non-dial results. Reserve `409` for true identity/fingerprint conflicts that prevent a frozen decision. |
| F-05 | accepted | Require current `service_activation.status = ACTIVE` for C10 and define a stable denial. Clarify C6 also requires current active activation and no other `ACTIVE`/`UNKNOWN` call before it creates a task. |
| F-06 | accepted | Add the minimal state-conditional create/replace operations for DRAFT catalog-version and trial-policy-version candidates, with no generic CRUD or independent offer withdrawal. Recompute endpoint counts and S2 scope. |
| F-07 | accepted | Give C9 deterministic sort keys and bind its opaque cursor to the normalized filter digest. |
| F-08 | accepted | Allow P3 an already-admitted, valid session while the grant is `LOCKED`; `LOCKED` blocks only new P2 admission. Preserve generation, expiry, risk and member-witness checks. |
| F-09 | accepted | The plan mandates `/api/v1/**` with `servers: /`, but all 49 inventory rows use unversioned paths. Update every operation path and route example to its exact `/api/v1/...` form, retain `servers: /`, and add a row-by-row/static audit that proves all five surface prefixes and no unversioned operation path remain. |
| F-10 | accepted | C6/C8 cannot reuse IVR decision reasons as HTTP Problem codes while the global registry forbids them. Define distinct customer-surface Problem codes for active-call conflict, outbound-task conflict and non-cancellable task state; remove `TASK_CONFLICT`/`CALL_IN_PROGRESS`/`TASK_CLAIMED`/`TASK_UNAVAILABLE` from C6/C8 Problem mappings and preserve those values only as IVR decision reasons. |
| F-11 | accepted | O18-O21 must not map to `platform.idempotency_record`: A0 fixes its scope registry to `IVR_CONFIRM` and `PURCHASE`. Remove that A0 mapping and the unsupported `Idempotency-Key` contract. Use state-conditional idempotency from the existing natural unique keys plus canonical stored-content comparison for creates, and `expectedRevision` plus requested/current content comparison for replacements; same semantic replay returns current/first DRAFT and changed content returns the existing stable DRAFT conflict/stale code. Do not add an A0 scope, table or transaction. |
| F-12 | accepted | Make O1 deterministic: `createdAt desc, subjectId desc`, bind the cursor to the normalized documented filter digest, and enumerate `CURSOR_INVALID`/`CURSOR_FILTER_MISMATCH`. Keep filters structured and exclude full-number search. |
| F-13 | accepted | Make path/count validation slice-aware. Shared Redocly rules must always enforce versioned approved prefixes and route uniqueness, while an explicit stage-aware check asserts the exact S1, S2 and final S3 operation sets/counts. Each slice must invoke its own reproducible check command; final 49 counts must not make S1/S2 fail. |

### A1 re-review clarification adjudication

- `servers` is required, not optional: the canonical document must contain exactly the approved relative root `url: /`; validation must reject missing, absolute or additional server roots.
- Inventory summaries must not use wildcard/bare error placeholders. Expand C4 `CLAIM_*`, C10 `PURCHASE_CONSENT_*`, and bare O4/O11/O14 409s into the exact approved external Problem codes used by the registry/docs/15.
- `RecordingDeclaration.upload.credential`, `ShareAdmission.mediaAuthorization.credential` and `MediaAuthorization.credential` are response credentials and must be `readOnly` plus `x-onecall-log-redaction: required`; no return-once claim is added unless the underlying replay semantics require it.
- For `ORGANIZATION_MEMBER`, P1 without a matching authenticated customer session may expose only verification/challenge metadata, not shared content metadata; P2/P3 retain the admitted recipient witness and tenant checks.
- The O20 provisional non-null `published_by_employee_id` is an A0 persistence mismatch, because A0 defines it as the actual publisher fact. A1 must not expose it, relabel it as draft creator, or claim the API solved the schema issue. Record it as a runtime prerequisite/residual owned by A0 schema/catalog-order before runtime trial-policy draft creation; the A1 work unit may still specify the public request/response contract and static mapping but must make no runtime claim.

### A1 bounded plan-fix requirements

- Resolve the review open questions within the plan where they are contract-shape decisions: P1 is observation-free; a P2 ticket failure after committed admission is fail-closed `503` and replay reissues without recounting; P3 is the credential-issuance/reconstruction control plane while the media gateway validates the credential and `ORGANIZATION_MEMBER` reissue remains bound to the admitted recipient witness.
- Correct OpenAPI secrecy annotations: response-only capability token/generated code use `readOnly` plus `x-onecall-return-once`; incoming verification secrets use `writeOnly`.
- Keep `limit` default `20` and max `100` only as explicitly labelled V1 API-governance defaults, owned by Backend/API governance rather than security or product policy.
- Recompute all operation and slice counts from the revised inventory. The intended bounded shape is customer 18, operations 21, IVR 6, public-share 3 and callback 1 (49 total), but the plan author must verify the exact inventory rather than treat this as a substitute for the calculation.
- Allowed plan-fix edits: `docs/gateflow/a1-production-openapi-surfaces-spec-plan.md` and one new `docs/gateflow/a1-production-openapi-surfaces-spec-plan-fix-*.md` artifact. No commits, push, PR, A0/source-doc/prototype/screenshot/review-artifact modification or later-gate action.
- F-09 is a bounded follow-up to plan-fix-1: all 49 inventory paths and route examples must use their exact `/api/v1/**` forms while `servers: /` remains unchanged. Static validation must audit the full set. It must not change operation counts, endpoint semantics, A0 mappings, slices or non-goals.

- Design source: `docs/12-technology-stack-and-system-architecture.md`, with current business rules referenced there in `docs/09-business-activation-and-lean-admin.md`, `docs/10-cross-channel-rules.md`, `docs/11-account-identity-and-workspaces.md`, and `docs/13-ivr-call-task-and-inbound-design.md`.
- Motivation: the repository contains reviewed prototypes and design artifacts but no production Maven project, database migrations or production service code. The architecture explicitly requires the production data model and Flyway initial schema specification before implementation.

## Proposed goal contract

- Goal: produce a code-generation-ready PostgreSQL 16 data model and initial Flyway schema specification covering the first production baseline for account/login identity, organization membership, employee identity, business subject/number binding, consent/activation, IVR task/session/call linkage, trial/entitlement/reservation/usage, catalog/order/payment, evidence/media metadata, sharing authorization, idempotency, Outbox and append-only audit facts.
- Success signal: every table and invariant has a single owning module; identifiers, sensitive-field handling, state/revision columns, foreign keys, uniqueness/concurrency constraints, immutable snapshots, deletion policy and migration order are explicit; the specification maps critical cross-channel and IVR state rules to database-enforceable or transaction-enforced controls; unresolved production inputs have an owner and destination instead of guessed values.
- Scope boundary: specification and validation only. The work unit may add the schema design document and its Gateflow artifacts/control updates.
- Non-goals: no executable Flyway migration, Maven/Spring project, API implementation, UI changes, cloud resources, vendor credentials, runtime deployment, production data import or speculative microservice split.
- No over-design: use the approved modular-monolith/PostgreSQL boundary and only model facts required by the current business baseline. Provider-specific configuration, capacity numbers, retention periods and future online workflows remain deferred.

## Project-level constraints

- PostgreSQL is the persistent business truth; Redis cannot own permanent authorization or entitlement facts.
- Module owners cannot write another module's tables directly.
- Existing HTML prototypes and `shared/onecall-business.js` are behavior/QA evidence, not a production schema or SDK.
- Natural-person accounts, login identities, organization memberships, business subjects/numbers and operations employees remain distinct.
- IVR task claim, rights reservation and frozen dial authorization are one transaction; a normalized caller has at most one non-terminal dial task.
- Trials, evidence, activation transitions, payment events, entitlement grants and idempotency records require stable uniqueness and immutable historical facts.
- Recording bytes remain in private object storage; PostgreSQL stores immutable object identity, version and hash metadata.
- Work orders, refunds and invoices remain offline support processes for this release.

## Slice principle

Implementation slices must be behaviorally verifiable and should minimize Gateflow review cost. The planning Agent must not split mechanically by file or table owner; if more than three slices are proposed, it must explain why they cannot be merged safely.

## Worktree boundary

The pre-existing modified QA screenshots and untracked `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261021-205118.md` are outside this project-execution work unit. They must remain unstaged and must not be changed, deleted or committed by Phaseflow Agents.

- Runtime/provider/task: Claude Code / `ds` / `a0-plan-claude-ds-20261022-01`.
- Result validation: runner exit code `0`; valid JSON; `subtype=success`; `is_error=false`; non-empty completion report; stderr empty.
- Controller validation: 18 required numbered sections, 3 slices, 43 table rows, 35 invariant rows and 9 migration-wave rows; zero multi-owner table or invariant rows; all invariants name `DB`, `TX` or `SERVICE`; both deferred cross-wave foreign keys are assigned; no trailing whitespace.
- Scope validation: the planning Agent changed only `docs/gateflow/a0-production-data-model-schema-spec-plan.md`; pre-existing screenshot changes, the old review artifact and controller remained outside the Agent write scope.
- Runtime switch: two earlier `codex-agent-run` attempts using the same `ds` provider wrote portions of the plan but failed after file-change tool calls with `No tool output found for tool call`. A read-only Claude/DeepSeek smoke test passed, so the successful bounded write gate used `claude-agent-run`. Do not retry the known-incompatible Codex/DeepSeek Responses write path without a verified adapter fix.
- Controller decision: accept the completed plan for plan review. The 43-table/35-invariant counts remain reviewable constraints rather than permission to add scope; any change requires a finding and controller adjudication against the confirmed goal.

### Plan review execution record

- Runtime/provider/task: Claude Code / `ds` / `a0-plan-review-claude-ds-20261022-02`.
- Initial dispatch failure: task `a0-plan-review-claude-ds-20261022-01` exited before model execution because the desktop process had not inherited the Windows user-level `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`; repository writes: none. The one permitted same-provider retry injected the user-level variable into only the WSL child environment and did not persist or print the credential.
- Result validation: retry exit code `0`; valid JSON; `subtype=success`; `is_error=false`; non-empty completion report; stderr empty; exactly one new artifact at `docs/reviews/plan-review-20261022-113039.md`.
- Skill conformance: the review Agent could not read the external skill path because its session restricted reads to the repository, but the Phaseflow controller read the complete `planreview` skill, supplied its required lenses/format/boundaries in the dispatch, and verified the artifact contains the mandated scope, assumptions, finding format, open questions, residual risks, all six lenses, slice conclusion and final conclusion. No re-run is required for this process limitation.
- Review conclusion: `fail` with 14 material findings. The plan remains the accepted plan-gate artifact but is not approved for implementation.

### Plan review finding adjudication

| Finding | Decision | Controller rationale and required destination |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PR-001 | accepted | The stated S1 -> S2 -> S3 order has real forward dependencies. Fix must produce an acyclic, behaviorally verifiable dependency graph; prefer three slices unless a fourth is proven necessary. |
| PR-002 | accepted | Activation plus first-trial issuance is required to be atomic, while trial/grant/balance belong to `entitlement-metering`. Fix must define an owner-preserving published service participating in the same transaction and its failure/replay tests. |
| PR-003 | accepted | Direct DTMF authorization lacks the canonical-caller lock and a proof that it is the no-task `ENTER_CALLEE` path. Fix must close both concurrency and trust-boundary gaps. |
| PR-004 | accepted | One order-level `entitlement_status` cannot own per-line/per-installment issuance state without a cross-module write. The controller authorizes a minimal `entitlement_metering.entitlement_issuance` fact/current-state table and a baseline increase from 43 to 44 tables if the fix confirms it is the smallest complete model. |
| PR-005 | accepted | D18/INV-35 claim event-domain uniqueness that the listed IVR and usage keys do not implement. Fix must make domain identity and allowed same-string cross-domain behavior explicit. |
| PR-006 | accepted | Activation replay lacks a request fingerprint/first-result binding and operations transitions lack a mandatory action-event identity. Fix must define both. |
| PR-007 | accepted | The `call_id` chain does not name FK parents or intentional deferred/no-FK cases. Fix must specify parent, creation timing and migration wave for each link. |
| PR-008 | accepted | Per-campaign uniqueness permits a second trial campaign despite the permanent `FIRST-TRIAL` rule. Fix must constrain the baseline first-trial identity and test alternate keys/version changes. |
| PR-009 | accepted | Evidence creation ownership is only one-way. Fix must specify the reciprocal creation-event constraint and atomic insertion behavior. |
| PR-010 | accepted | A committed admission followed by Redis failure has no durable retry-key/ticket reconstruction contract. Fix must preserve count-once semantics without making Redis business truth. |
| PR-011 | accepted | A missing authoritative call-end event can strand or incorrectly release a reservation. Fix must define reconciliation states/fields, owner, late-event behavior and tests. |
| PR-012 | accepted | Count checks do not prove catalog-to-wave-to-slice completeness, uniqueness, lifecycle coverage or FK order. Fix must add deterministic structural validation with named missing/extra output. |
| PR-013 | accepted | `catalog_product` cannot be both append-only/frozen and a mutable draft-deletion exception. Fix must choose one lifecycle contract and align deletion/revision validation. |
| PR-014 | accepted | The chosen DB-enforced ownership model depends on cross-schema functions. Fix must either specify a safe, atomic privilege/function contract or simplify the enforcement claim; implementation must not guess `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path`, grants or replay/redaction behavior. |

The 43-table and 35-invariant counts were plan-internal review constraints, not Goal Confirmation terms. The fix may change those counts only as required by accepted findings and must update all counts, catalogs, waves, slices and validation assertions consistently. No other scope expansion is authorized.

- Artifacts: revised plan plus `docs/gateflow/a0-production-data-model-schema-spec-plan-fix.md`; no other task-owned write.

- Controller structural check: 18 required sections; 44 unique baseline-matrix tables; slice distribution 13/23/8; lifecycle distribution 27 mutable and 17 append-only; 40 invariants with one owner and an enforcement class; 81 FK/no-FK decisions with no parent-later-than-FK wave; 14 fix status rows all marked `已修复`; no stale 43/35 count, `entitlement_status` token or trailing whitespace.
- Mandatory re-review checks: (1) the standalone global table-count command currently also matches three cross-schema function rows and therefore does not yield 44 unless scoped to the catalog; (2) `operations_audit.append_fact` must be reconciled with the actual `audit` schema; (3) `platform.idempotency_begin` must have a complete first-result persistence/finalization contract rather than returning an unfillable result; (4) the evidence creation-event FK must be valid PostgreSQL against the composite domain/event uniqueness, not a single-column FK to a non-unique target; (5) every original finding must be assigned one of the allowed final re-review states based on direct evidence.

Non-destructive inspection proved no network connection, empty result/stderr and no artifact; the misconfigured process was terminated and retried once with an explicit option terminator.

- Re-review conclusion: `fail`. Original finding states: 9 `已修复`, 5 `部分修复`, 0 `未修复`, 0 `证据失效`.

### Fix round 2 requirements

| Finding | Decision | Required correction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F-01 / PR-001 | accepted | Move `entitlement_metering.issue_first_trial` creation and verification to V006/S2 after its tables exist. V001/S1 verifies only platform/audit functions. |
| F-02 / PR-006 / PR-014 | accepted | Add a complete, same-transaction, write-once `platform.idempotency_complete` contract; make the idempotency row explicitly finalizable exactly once, define concurrent `NEW/REPLAY/CONFLICT` behavior and rollback semantics, and update lifecycle/validation consistently. |
| F-03 / PR-009 | accepted | Replace the invalid single-column evidence FK with a PostgreSQL-valid composite `(source_event_domain, source_event_id)` to `(event_domain, event_id)` FK. Because evidence record/event are cyclic, specify valid same-wave creation order and the exact `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraints/trigger. |
| F-04 / PR-014 | accepted | Rename/rehome the audit function consistently as `audit.append_fact`, owned by the `operations-audit` role with `search_path = audit, pg_temp`; no undeclared `operations_audit` schema. |
| F-05 | accepted | Bind order creation to `request_fingerprint` and the first immutable order result under the `PURCHASE` idempotency scope; same key/different input conflicts and identical replay returns the first order. |
| F-06 / PR-014 | accepted | Persist the first-trial source action domain, action event ID and request fingerprint in an entitlement-owned row. Define same-action replay, changed-fingerprint conflict and already-granted-by-another-action behavior without reissuing trial/grant/balance. |
| F-07 / PR-012 | accepted | Scope table counting to the catalog; compare catalog exactly against rendered §12 wave membership and §13 slice membership; validate every FK's referenced column set against a machine-readable PK/UNIQUE key catalog; distinguish ordinary, late-added and truly deferrable FKs; run the same structural checks on the future final spec. |

Fix round 2 must create a new durable fix artifact and preserve the first fix/re-review artifacts as historical evidence. It may update counts such as lifecycle/FK modes only as a direct consequence of these accepted corrections, and must update every dependent assertion consistently.

The command exited `1`, not pass: its section 11 function-wave `awk` program has a syntax error because the `n=split(...)` and subsequent dependency loop are not enclosed in an action block. The composite evidence FK and candidate-key/FK-target checks in section 8 of the embedded script ran without errors before section 11.

No plan or fix is made during this gate.
